## EXPLANATORY STATEMENT

This Bill seeks to consolidate the Securities Industry Act 1983 ("Act 280") and the Futures Industry Act 1993 ("Act 499"), and the relevant provisions in relation to fund raising activities under the Securities Commission Act 1993 ("Act 498"), and to provide for matters incidental thereto and connected therewith.

The scope of the proposed Act is as follows: • to introduce a single licensing framework for capital market intermediaries; • to promote Malaysia as a global Islamic financial hub; • to modernise the regulatory framework in relation to financial markets and products; • to streamline fund raising activities and strengthen the protection of investors; and • to provide for the establishment and the supervisory framework for self- regulatory organizations in the capital market.

2. Clause 1 contains the short title and the power of the Minister to appoint different commencement dates for different provisions of this proposed Act.

3. Clause 2 seeks to define certain words and expressions used in the proposed Act. Some of the salient definitions are as follows:

the definition of "approved clearing house" which shall be an approved clearing house in place of an approved clearing house under Act 499 and a recognized clearing house under Act 280.

the definitions of "Capital Markets Services Licence", "Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence" and "regulated activity" have been introduced in order to give effect to the single licensing framework which is one of the main thrusts of the proposed Act. "Regulated activities" for the purposes of the proposed Act is further described in Schedule 2.

the definition of "self-regulatory organization" incorporates a framework that provides for the recognition and oversight of a recognized self-regulatory organization.

4. Clause 3 deals with the term "associated person". It seeks to set out a list of persons who shall be deemed as persons associated with another person.

<!-- page 447 -->

5. Clause 4 deals with the term "interest in securities". It seeks to set out a list of what constitutes interest in securities.

6. Clause 5 seeks to provide for the prescription of securities and futures contracts.

7. Clause 6 seeks to provide for the consequences of agreements becoming a futures contract.

8. Clause 7 seeks to provide for the establishment of stock market, futures market, exempt stock market and exempt futures market. This clause also introduces a new category of markets known as "registered electronic facility".

9. Clause 8 seeks to provide for the power of the Minister to approve a stock exchange and a futures exchange.

10. Clause 9 seeks to provide for the power of the Commission to approve any amendments made to the rules of the stock exchange, the futures exchange and an approved clearing house.

11. Clause 10 seeks to establish a public interest framework in relation to the composition of the board of directors of an exchange holding company, a stock exchange and a futures exchange. This clause provides that the boards of these market institutions must comprise of a specified number of public interest directors that are to be appointed by the Minister.

12. Clause 11 seeks to provide for the duties of the stock exchange and futures exchange.

13. Clause 12 empowers the Minister to withdraw an approval given to a stock exchange or futures exchange pursuant to clause 8. The effect of the withdrawal of such approval is provided for in clause 13.

14. Clause 14 seeks to provide that an exchange holding company or a holding company of an exchange holding company may only be established with the approval of the Minister. The approval process that must be complied with in order to establish an exchange holding company is provided for in clause 15.

15. Clause 16 requires a stock exchange, a futures exchange and an exchange holding company to submit to the Commission an annual regulatory report.

16. Clause 17 empowers the Minister to require a stock exchange, a futures exchange or an exchange holding company to prepare and submit to him at any time a special report on their compliance with the requirements of the proposed Act, and any provisions of the securities laws and rules.

17. Clause 18 empowers the Minister to withdraw an approval given to an exchange holding company. The effect of such withdrawal is provided in clause 19.

<!-- page 448 -->

18. Clause 20 seeks to provide for the listing of the exchange holding company on the stock exchange.

19. Clause 21 seeks to set out the various duties of an exchange holding company.

20. Clause 22 imposes the requirement of having to establish a Risk Management Committee on an exchange holding company.

21. Clause 23 imposes restrictions on an exchange holding company from reducing its shareholdings in its subsidiaries.

22. Clause 24 seeks to require an exchange holding company, a stock exchange, a futures exchange, an approved clearing house, a central depository and any other relevant body corporate to notify the Commission of its intention to dispose or acquire assets that may have been specified by the Commission.

23. Clause 25 imposes restriction on acquiring a substantial shareholding in an exchange holding company.

24. Clause 26 empowers the Commission to issue directions to the exchange holding company, stock exchange, futures exchange, approved clearing house, central depository and any other relevant body corporate to deal with matters of conflict of interest.

25. Clause 27 provides for the procedure for the closure of the stock exchange or futures exchange in the case of an emergency.

26. Clause 28 empowers the Commission to prohibit trading on the stock exchange or futures exchange.

27. Clause 29 requires an exchange holding company, a stock exchange, a futures exchange and an approved clearing house to provide such assistance to the Commission as it may reasonably require.

28. Clause 30 seeks to enable the Minister to issue suspension orders on an. exchange holding company, a stock exchange, a futures exchange, an approved clearing house or a central depository for the purpose of protecting investors.

29. Clause 31 seeks to set out the various actions that the Commission can take upon contravention of clause 23, 24 or 25 of the proposed Act.

30. Clause 32 empowers the Commission to publish a notice in relation to any action taken by the Minister or the Commission under clause 12, 18, 27, 354 or 355 of the proposed Act.

31. Clause 33 seeks to provide that the rights of a stock exchange, a futures exchange, an approved clearing house or parties to a futures contract shall not be affected by any written laws relating to a contract.

<!-- page 449 -->

32. Clause 34 seeks to introduce a new provision to enable the Commission to register an electronic facility, This provision also enables the Commission to impose terms and conditions on the operator of the electronic facility to ensure that an orderly and fair market exists in relation to transactions that are carried out through the registered electronic facility. The registration process for the electronic facility is provided for in clause 35.

33. Clause 36 empowers the Commission to withdraw the registered status that has been accorded to an electronic facility and that the Commission may do so if, among others, it is in the interest of investors or for the maintenance of an orderly and fair market.

34. Clause 37 seeks to prohibit the provision of unauthorised clearing facilities. Unauthorised clearing facilities refers to clearing facilities that have not been approved by the Commission.

35. Clause 38 seeks to provide for the approval process that must be complied with in order to provide clearing house facilities. This clause enables the Commission, with the concurrence of the Minister, to approve a clearing house. The withdrawal of an approval accorded to an approved clearing house is provided for in clause 39. The effect of withdrawal of an approval is specified in clause 40.

36. Clauses 41 to 57 seek to modify the law of insolvency and other miscellaneous provisions relating to the operations and procedures of an approved clearing house.

37. Clause 58 seeks to require a capital market intermediary to hold a Capital Markets Services Licence in order to carry on a business in any of the regulated activities specified in Schedule 2 of the proposed Act. This clause seeks to give effect to the single licensing regime. Hence, intermediaries will only require one licence to carry on activities in the securities and futures industry. This clause also provides that specified persons listed out in Schedule 3 do not need to hold a Capital Markets Services Licence.

38. Clause 59 seeks to require a representative of a capital market intermediary to also hold a Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence.

39. Clause 60 seeks to provide for the application procedure for the grant or renewal of a Capital Markets Services Licence and Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence.

40. Clause 61 empowers the Commission to grant or renew a Capital Markets Services Licence or Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence. In the case of granting or renewing a Capital Markets Services Licence for carrying on a business of dealing in securities or trading in futures contracts, the clause provides that the Commission, with the concurrence of the Minister, may grant or renew such a licence.

41. Clause 62 empowers the Commission to impose conditions or restrictions on the licences that the Commission issues provided that in the case of a Capital Markets Services Licence for carrying on a business of dealing in

<!-- page 450 -->

securities or trading in futures contracts, conditions can only be imposed with the concurrence of the Minister.

42. Clause 63 seeks to provide for the payment of a prescribed licence fee and that the licence fee collected by the Commission shall be paid into the Federal Consolidated Fund.

43. Clause 64 seeks to provide a transparent criteria in assessing whether an applicant for a Capital Markets Services Licence is fit and proper. Clause 65 on the other hand specifies fit and proper criteria in relation to an applicant for a Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence.

44. Clause 66 empowers the Commission to enquire into transactions in respect of securities and futures contracts.

45. Clause 67 seeks to provide that holders of a Capital Markets Services Licence shall meet the minimum financial requirements as specified by the Commission before it can carry on any regulated activity.

46. Clause 68 spells out the duration of a licence.

47. Clause 69 empowers the Commission to vary a Capital Markets Services Licence or Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence.

48. Clause 70 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence carrying on a business of dealing in securities (other than participating organization) or fund management to lodge a deposit of RM 150,000 with the Commission or such other amount as the Minister may prescribe or take up insurance for the indemnification of claims up to an amount of RM 150,000 or such other amount, as the Minister may prescribe.

49. Clause 71 prohibits the making of false statements in relation to the application for the grant, renewal or variation of the Capital Markets Services Licence or Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence.

50. Clause 72 empowers the Commission to revoke or suspend a Capital Markets Services Licence or Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence. The effect of revocation, suspension or expiry of a Capital Markets Services Licence is provided for in clause 73.

51. Clause 74 requires a licensed person to notify the Commission of any disqualifying event.

52. Clause 75 introduces a new provision to enable a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence to appoint its directors and chief executive and to notify the Commission of the appointment, election and nomination of such persons. Responsibility is placed on the licence holder as well as on the individual who is to be appointed as a director or chief executive to ensure that he is fit and proper. In the event the person appointed as a director or chief executive is not fit and proper and continues to hold office, the Commission may issue a direction to the licence holder to remove that director or chief executive.

<!-- page 451 -->

53. Clause 76 introduces a new category of intermediaries who are allowed to carry on capital market activities without having to hold a Capital Markets Services Licence. These persons are referred to as "registered persons" and includes persons and institutions that are specified in Schedule 4.

54. Clause 77 empowers the Commission to establish and maintain a register of holders of a Capital Markets Services Licence and Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence.

55. Clause 78 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence or Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence to notify the Commission of any change in material particulars.

56. Clause 79 enables the Commission to publish the names and addresses of all licence persons.

57. Clause 80 provides that an appeal can be made to the Minister against the decision of the Commission in its refusal to grant, revoke or suspend a Capital Markets Services Licence.

58. Clause 81 sets out the process for surrendering the Capital Markets Services Licence or Capital Markets Services Representative's Licence to the Commission.

59. Clause 82 sets out the persons to whom Division 2 of Part III of the proposed Act shall apply which includes a licensed person who carries on the business of either dealing in securities, fund management, advising on corporate finance, investment advice or financial planning, financial journalist and an authorised depository agent. In clause 83, such persons shall establish and maintain a register of securities whereby particulars of the securities in which the specified person has an interest in must be entered into the register. Such persons shall notify the Commission of the place at which the register of securities is maintained as provided for in clause 84.

60. Clause 85 seeks to provide for a defence to prosecution in relation to cases involving a contravention of clause 83 or 84 of the proposed Act.

61. Clause 86 deals with of the requirement to produce the register of securities for inspection purposes.

62. Clause 87 enables the Commission to require certain persons to supply the Commission with particulars of financial journalist.

63. Clause 88 enables the Commission to supply a copy of the extract of the register of securities to any person on payment of a specified fee.

64. Clause 89 prohibits against the making of certain representations by a licensed person.

65. Clause 90 empowers the Minister to make regulations in relation to the issuance of contract notes.

<!-- page 452 -->

66. Clause 91 requires a licensed person to disclose its interest in any securities that it has recommended.

67. Clause 92 requires a licensed person to have a reasonable basis for providing advise or making recommendation to a client.

68. Clause 93 requires a licensed person who carries on the business of dealing in securities or fund management to give priority to its client order.

69. Clause 94 seeks to set out a restriction on dealings in securities by employees of a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence or of a participating organization.

70. Clause 95 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence to submit to the Commission returns and any information relating to its business as may be required by the Commission.

71. Clause 96 empowers the Commission to impose additional obligations on a licensed person.

72. Clause 97 provides for the prohibition against dealing as a principal on holders of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities.

73. Clause 98 provides for the general prohibition against shortselling as well as the situations in which the prohibition would not apply.

74. Clause 99 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of trading in futures contracts to not knowingly take the other side of an order of a client unless the client has consented to the taking of that order.

75. Clause 100 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of trading in futures contracts or fund management in respect of futures contracts to provide its prospective client with a risk disclosure document or any other information as may be specified by the Commission.

76. Clause 101 empowers the Commission to fix positions and trading limits in futures contracts.

77. Clause 102 empowers the Commission to fix or vary the reportable positioning in relation to futures contracts.

78. Clause 103 specifically provides that a futures contract shall not be taken to be a gaming or wagering contract.

79. Clause 104 sets out the requirement for the sequencing of sending and carrying out of orders in respect of trading in futures contracts.

80. Clause 105 provides for a general prohibition against trading in futures contracts on a futures market that is outside Malaysia unless trading is on a

<!-- page 453 -->

futures market of a specified exchange or trading is in respect of an approved class of futures contracts. This clause allows the futures exchange to specify exchanges and to approve the class of futures contracts.

81. Clause 106 provides for the requirement that in order to carry on trading in futures contracts on a specified exchange, such holders would have to pay to the Commission a specified amount that would be applied to deal with prescribed events such as the insolvency of the holder.

82. Clause 107 imposes criminal liability on persons who contravene clauses 99, 100, 101, 102 and 104 of the proposed Act.

83. Clause 108 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence to maintain such records that can explain the transactions and financial position of its business.

84. Clauses 109 and 110 provide that the application of Subdivision 2 of Division 4 of Part III of the proposed Act is confined to a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities and defines terms such as "client assets" for the purposes of the Subdivision.

85. Clause 111 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities to place monies received into a trust account.

86. Clause 112 specifies how client's assets received (other than in the form of monies received) by a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities are to be dealt with.

87. Clause 113 sets out the circumstances in which a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities may withdraw monies from a trust account.

88. Clause 114 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities to supply copies of entries in its books when requested by its client.

89. Clause 115 deals with the protection provided for monies held in a trust account against other claims or liens.

90. Clauses 116 and 117 provide that the application of Subdivision 3 of Division 4 of Part III of the proposed Act is confined to a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of trading in futures contracts and define terms such as "client assets", "property" and "client" for the purposes of the Subdivision.

91. Clause 118 provides that a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of trading in futures contracts must maintain a client's segregated account for monies and assets received from its client. Clause 119 states that monies or assets in the client's segregated account shall not be used to pay off any debt or liability of the holder of the Licence.

<!-- page 454 -->

92. Clauses 120 and 121 provide that the application of Subdivision 4 of Division 4 of Part III of the proposed Act is confined to a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of fund management and defines terms such as "trust account", "client's assets" and "custodian" for the purposes of the Subdivision.

93. Clause 122 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of fund management to appoint a custodian that will maintain a trust account. Client's monies and assets received by such holder shall be paid or deposited into the trust account.

94. Clause 123 specifies how client's assets received by a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of fund management are to be dealt with.

95. Clause 124 requires a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of fund management to supply its client with copies of entries in his book relating to the client's transaction.

96. Clause 125 sets out actions that may be taken by the Commission for the purpose of protecting client's assets or client's interest when the licensed person or an approved trustee has contravened the proposed Act or any guidelines.

97. Clause 126 spells out the requirement for the appointment of auditors and the prohibition against the appointment of associates as auditors by relevant persons. The provision further specifies that relevant persons are holders of a Capital Markets Services Licence, an exchange holding company, a stock exchange, a futures exchange, an approved clearing house, a central depository and a recognised self-regulatory organization. In clause 127, the relevant person shall lodge an auditor's report with the Commission within three months of the close of each financial year or such further period as may be specified by the Commission.

98. Clause 128 sets out the duties of the auditors to report to the Commission, the stock exchange or the futures exchange, as the case may be, of any breach of the proposed Act, the securities laws or any irregularity that may have a material effect on the accounts of the relevant person.

99. Clause 129 requires a relevant person or any of its directors to provide the auditors with relevant information.

100. Clause 130 empowers the Commission to appoint independent auditors in the event the relevant person has failed to lodge an auditor's report with the Commission as required by clause 127 or where the Commission has received a report under clause 127 or 128.

101. Clause 131 empowers the Commission to appoint an independent auditor upon the application of a client of a relevant person.

102. Clause 132 requires an independent auditor who is appointed by the Commission under clause 130 or 131 to report to the Commission his findings upon the conclusion of his examination.

<!-- page 455 -->

103. Clause 133 provides for the powers of an independent auditor appointed by the Commission. The powers include examination of persons and employing such persons as may be necessary to carry out the examination for which the independent auditor was appointed for.

104. Clause 134 prohibits independent auditors from communicating certain matters to any persons other than the Commission.

105. Clause 135 deals with the powers of an independent auditor to request for books, accounts and records of a relevant person.

106. Clause 136 sets out the penalty for destroying, concealing or altering books.

107. Clause 137 spells out the right of a stock exchange or a futures exchange to impose further obligations on a participating organization or a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of trading in futures contracts in relation to audit of accounts.

108. Clauses 138 and 139 seek to facilitate the transfer of business by one licence holder to another by way of a vesting order from the court.

109. Clauses 140 to 158 provide for the establishment, contribution, maintenance and application of the compensation fund by a stock exchange. The compensation fund is to be applied to compensate persons who have suffered monetary loss as a result of the insolvency of the participating organization or as a result of any defalcation or fraudulent misuse of monies or other property by a director, officer, employee or representative of a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries the business of dealing in securities and who is also a participating organization of the stock exchange.

110. Clauses 159 to 173 provide for the establishment, contribution, maintenance and application of the fidelity fund by a futures exchange. The fidelity fund is to be applied to compensate persons who have suffered monetary loss as a result of any defalcation or fraudulent misuse of monies or other property by a director, officer, employee or representative of a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence who carries on the business of trading in futures contracts and who is an affiliate of the futures exchange.

111. Clause 174 deals with the application of Divisions 1 and 2 of Part V. Division 1 sets out the various market offences in respect of the securities while Division 2 sets out the various market offences in respect of futures contract.

112. Clauses 175, 176 and 777 deal with the prohibition against false trading and market rigging transactions, stock market manipulations and the making of false and misleading statements respectively.

113. Clauses 178 and 179 set out the prohibition against fraudulently inducing persons to deal in securities and the use of manipulative and deceptive devices in connection with the subscription, purchase or sale of securities respectively.

<!-- page 456 -->

114. Clause 180 empowers the Minister to prescribe and make regulations to exempt any persons from the application of clause 175 or 776.

115. Clause 181 prohibits the dissemination of information about illegal transactions.

116. Clause 182 provides for the penalty for offences committed against clause 175, 176, 177, 178, 179 or 181.

117. Clauses 183 to 198 deal with the prohibition against insider trading. The clauses define various terms that are relevant for the offence of insider trading and the defences that are available.

118. Clause 199 sets out the civil liability for the contravention of clause 175, 176, 177, 178, 179 or 181. It provides that any person who suffers loss or damage as a result of the conduct of another person who has contravened any of the provisions may recover that loss or damage by instituting civil proceedings against that other person.

119. Clause 200 provides for civil proceedings for the contravention of clause 175, 176, 177, 178, 179 or 181. It provides that the Commission may institute civil proceedings against a person who has contravened any of the provisions although that person may not have been charged with an offence. The Commission may recover an amount which shall not exceed three times the gross amount of pecuniary gain made or loss avoided by the person in breach. It also allows the Commission to claim a civil penalty against the person in breach for an amount not exceeding one million ringgit.

120. Clause 201 enables any person who suffer losses or damages as a result of the conduct of another person who has contravened clause 188 to recover that loss or damages by instituting civil proceedings against that person.

121. Clauses 202 to 209 set out the various market offences in relation to the futures contract. These clauses respectively provide for the offence of false trading, bucketing, manipulation of price of futures contract, employment of devices to defraud and making a false or misleading statement to induce another to enter into a futures contract.

122. Clause 210 deals with civil liability for the contravention of clause 202, 203, 204, 205, 206, 207 or 208 It provides that any person who suffers loss or damage as a result of the conduct of another person who has contravened any of the said provisions may recover that loss or damage by instituting civil proceedings against that person.

123. Clause 211 provides for civil proceedings for the contravention of clauses 202, 203, 204, 205, 206, 207 or 208. It provides that the Commission may institute civil proceedings against a person who has contravened any of the said provisions although that person may not be charged with an offence. This clause provides that the Commission may recover an amount which shall not exceed three times the gross amount of pecuniary gain made or loss avoided by the person in breach. It also allows the Commission to claim a civil penalty against the person for an amount not exceeding one million ringgit.

<!-- page 457 -->

124. Clauses 212 to 316 relate to the fund raising provisions which have been transferred from Part IV of the Act 498. This inclusion would ensure that the substantive provisions regulating the securities and futures industry are provided for in the proposed Act.

125. Clause 317 deals with the chief executive and directors of a listed corporation who have an interest in the securities of the listed corporation shall notify the listed corporation in writing of his interest and extent of his interest in the securities unless exempted by the Commission.

126. Clause 318 provides for the disqualification of an unfit person to be the chief executive or director of a listed corporation.

127. Clause 319 requires a listed corporation to submit to the Commission a copy of its audited annual accounts and its interim and periodic financial reports.

128. Clause 320 provides for the duties of an auditor of a listed corporation.

129. Clause 321 provides for the protection of persons against any retaliation for reporting to the Commission or to the stock exchange of any breach or non- performance of any requirement or provisions of the proposed Act or securities laws or any rules of the stock exchange.

130. Clause 322 defines the term "chief executive", "director" and "officer" in respect of a self-regulatory organization.

131. Clause 323 provides for the power of the Commission, with the concurrence of the Minister, to recognize a self-regulatory organization. This clause also sets out the minimum conditions that must be satisfied before a self-regulatory organization can be accorded with recognised status.

132. Clause 324 provides for the duties of a recognized self-regulatory organization while clause 325 provides that such organization cannot amend its rules without prior approval of the Commission.

133. Clause 326 states that a recognized self-regulatory organization shall not appoint a person as its chief executive or director without prior approval of the Commission.

134. Clause 327 confers on the Commission the power to issue directions to a recognized self-regulatory organization when there is a conflict between the interest of the organization and its duties under the proposed Act.

135. Clause 328 confers on the Commission the power, with the concurrence of the Minister, to withdraw the recognized status that has been accorded on to a self- regulatory organization.

136. Clause 329 states that a recognized self-regulatory organization, its officers and employees will not be liable for anything done or omitted to be done in the performance of their duties or functions as a recognized self-regulatory organization.

<!-- page 458 -->

137. Clause 330 provides that the audit of a recognized self-regulatory organization shall be carried out in accordance to the provisions of Subdivision 6 of Division 4 of Part III of the proposed Act.

138. Clauses 332 to 346 deal with the provisions relating to the Capital Market Development Fund. These clauses seek to provide for the establishment, objects and administration of the Capital Market Development Fund. The Fund shall be administered by the Capital Market Development Fund Board whose members shall be appointed by the Minister.

139. Clause 347 deals with the application of Part X of the proposed Act which provides for the disclosure of information to the Commission in order to enable the Commission to enforce the provisions of the proposed Act. Its provisions, however, do not extend to licensed institutions and Islamic banks but would apply to a licensed institution that holds a Capital Markets Services Licence.

140. Clause 348 confers on the Commission the power to require the production of books. The penalty for the contravention of the requirement mentioned in clause 348 is provided for in clause 349.

141. Clause 350 empowers the Commission to specify the form and manner in which the information, returns or documents are required to be submitted to the Commission and the period for that submission.

142. Clause 351 sets out the protection provided for an advocate and solicitor in relation to privileged information.

143. Clause 352 confers the power to the Commission to require a holder of a Capital Market Services Licence who carries on the business of dealing in securities or trading in futures contracts to disclose to the Commission specified information in relation to such dealing or trading.

144. Clause 353 provides for the power of the Commission to require any person to disclose to the Commission information relating to dealing in securities or trading in futures contracts.

145. Clauses 354 and 355 confer the power to the Commission to take various administrative actions against persons that are specified in those clauses.

146. Clause 356 provides for the power of the Commission to take administrative actions against a licensed person for contravening or failing to comply with any requirement, or provision of any securities laws, written notice, guidelines or conditions or restrictions imposed on the licence granted to the licensed person.

147. Clause 357 sets out the civil liability for the contravention of Part VI of the proposed Act. It provides that any person who suffers loss or damage as a result of the conduct of another person who has contravened any of the

<!-- page 459 -->

provisions of Part VI or any regulations made under the proposed Act, may recover that loss or damage by instituting civil proceeding against that person. Clause 358 relates to the power of the Commission to recover loss or damage on behalf of other persons who have suffered loss by the conduct of another person who has contravened that Part.

148. Clause 359 clarifies the reference to conduct in relation to the activities under

## Part VI of the proposed Act.

149. Clause 360 empowers the court to make certain orders if applied for by, among others, the Commission, the exchange holding company, a stock exchange, a futures exchange and an approved clearing house.

150. Clause 361 provides the authority to the Commission, a stock exchange, a futures exchange or an approved clearing house to apply to the court to wind up a company under specified grounds.

151. Clause 362 prohibits the use of certain titles such as "participating organization", "exchange holding company", "futures exchange", etc. It also prohibits a non-licensed person from using a title or description implying that the person is a licensed person.

152. Clause 363 relates to evidence obtained from a copy of the book maintained by a stock exchange, a futures exchange or a holder of a Capital Markets Services Licence.

153. Clause 364 provides a means for an aggrieved party to apply to the Commission to have the Commission review its own decisions made pursuant to the proposed Act. Clause 365 provides for the time that the application referred to in clause 364 must be made.

154. Clause 366 provides that any decision made by the Minister under the proposed Act shall be final.

155. Clause 367 clarifies the relationship between a body corporate and its officers in relation to offences.

156. Clause 368 prohibits the falsification of records while clause 369 prohibits the making and submission of any false report to the Commission, stock exchange, futures exchange or an approved clearing house.

157. Clause 370 deals with a provision for attempts, abetments and conspiracies in relation to offences under the proposed Act.

158. Clause 371 prohibits against the destruction, concealment, mutilation and alteration of records by any person.

159. Clause 372 provides for a general penalty for the proposed Act.

160. Clause 373 provides that the Commission, with the consent of the Public Prosecutor, may compound any offence committed by a person against Part II, HI, VI, VII, X or XII of the proposed Act, or any regulations made thereunder.

<!-- page 460 -->

161. Clause 374 requires persons convicted under Part V or VI of the proposed Act to pay compensation as may be determined by the court to any person who has suffered loss or damage as a result of the offence committed by the convicted person.

162. Clause 375 specifically states that no prosecution for an offence under the proposed Act may be instituted without a written consent from the Public Prosecutor.

163. Clause 376 provides that no civil liability shall be incurred by a stock exchange, a futures exchange, an exchange holding company, an approved clearing house or a central depository and by its officers for anything done, or any statement made in connection with the discharge of any of its duties under the proposed Act, the securities laws or its rules.

164. Clause 377 empowers the Commission to issue guidelines and practice notes.

165. Clause 378 provides for the power of the Minister to make regulations. It also provides that the Minister may make regulations pertaining to matters specified in Schedule 10 of the proposed Act.

166. Clause 379 provides that the rules of a stock exchange, a futures exchange, a central depository or an approved clearing house may make provisions for the settlement of disputes. The clause also provides that the Minister can make regulations for the settlement of disputes.

167. Clause 380 provides for the power of the Minister to amend the Schedules to the proposed Act.

168. Clause 381 provides for the repeal of Act 280 and Act 499, saving provision of any regulations, directions, notifications, exemptions, orders, approvals, decisions, applications and transactions that were issued or entered into under both the repealed Acts.

169. Clause 382 saves the approvals granted to the various market institutions that have been approved or recognised under the repealed Act 280 and Act 499 and preserves any conditions or restrictions that have been imposed by the Minister when approving or recognising these institutions.

170. Clause 383 states that the electronic broking system which was accorded exempt stock market status under the Securities Industry (Declaration of Exempt Stock Market) Order 2005 shall be deemed to be a registered electronic facility for the purposes of the proposed Act.

171. Clauses 384 and 385 respectively relate to the preservation of the various intermediary licences that have been issued under the repealed Acts 280 and Act 499 and the applications made for intermediary licences under both Acts.

172. Clause 386 deals with saving provisions relating to rules, orders, approvals, decisions and regulations issued or made pursuant to Part IV of Act 498. The insertion of this clause is necessary given the fact that Part IV of Act 498 has been transferred into Part VI of the proposed Act.

<!-- page 461 -->

173. Clauses 387 and 388 relate to the revocation of specific subsidiary legislation as specified. Such revocation is necessary as they have been incorporated into the specific Schedules to the proposed act or may no longer be relevant with the coming into effect of the proposed Act.

174. Clauses 389 to 391 provide for the modifications to the construction of other written laws.

175. Clause 392 provides that persons dealing in securities in relation to unit trust scheme that are members of a body approved by the Commission under the repealed Act 280 shall for the purposes of clause 76 of the proposed Act be treated as registered persons.

176. Clause 393 provides for a transition period of one year to enable unlicensed unit trust management companies to be licensed under the proposed Act.

177. Clause 394 relates to provisional provisions for corporate finance executives and research analysts.

## FINANCIAL IMPLICATIONS

This Bill will not involve the Government in any extra financial expenditure.
